§3.1.1 — Effect context, track, recover
  ✓ Theorem 4: pr₁ ∘ track(f, g) = f ∘ pr₁ — tracking is transparent to the forward state
  ✓ Theorem 5: track is a monoid homomorphism — track(p₁ ∘ p₂) = track(p₁) ∘ track(p₂)
  ✓ Theorem 7 / eq. 11: recover ∘ track = recover; a tracked sequence recovers (γ₀, id)
  ✓ Soundness invariant: φ(γ) = γ₀ at every state a tracked sequence reaches
§3.1.2 — Effect functions, ⋄, the lift to ∂Γ
  ✓ Definition 8: effects choose their inverse per state, and are witnessed there
  ✓ Theorem 10: (𝔈_Γ, ⋄) is a monoid — associativity and unit η
  ✓ Theorem 11: 𝔈*_Γ is a submonoid — witnessing survives ⋄
  ✓ Theorem 14: the lift agrees with the effect one level down — pr₁ ∘ f′ = f ∘ pr₁
  ✓ Theorem 15: the lifted inverse recovers the state exactly — g′(Δ) = (γ, φ ∘ g ∘ f)
  ✓ Theorem 16: LIFO revert recovers every intermediate state, invariant throughout
§3.1.3 — Independence and out-of-order withdrawal
  ✓ Definition 19: key-disjoint effects are independent; same-key effects are not
  ✓ Corollary 21: pairwise independent effects revert to γ₀ under every permutation
§3.2.1 — The coeffect context and its operations
  ✓ Definition 22: preconditions — no double provision, no absent revocation, no absent read
  ✓ Definition 23: set(k, v) is a witnessed effect on Σ — 𝔈*_Σ
  ✓ eq. 23: an operation lift acts on the binding alone; its inverse maps the value it then finds
  ✓ eq. 22: outcomes are paired with each application, not with the lift
  ✓ Independence in Σ: operation lifts at distinct keys are independent (Definition 19)
  ✓ Independence tolerates partiality: an undefined sample point yields no generator
§3.2.2 — Satisfaction and notification
  ✓ eq. 24: σ ⊨ d — satisfaction is membership of every declared key

§3.3.2 — Observational equivalence and commutative keys
  ✓ Definition 33: states relate when they bind the same keys to ≈_k-related values
  ✓ Definition 39 / Theorem 40 flavor: a table-valued key is commutative; an ordered chain is not
The reactive runtime — failure and precondition discipline (§3.2.1, §3.2.2)
  ✓ A failed activation produces no transition: effect rolled back, component inactive
  ✓ Mounting an already-mounted name is refused: its accumulator must never be orphaned
  ✓ A component named "host" is an ordinary component, not the host pseudo-entry

Hot module replacement, reactively

The capstone: a logger, a metrics component that needs the logger, a dashboard that needs both. Nobody wires anything. One provision activates the tree; unmounting the logger deactivates its dependents before the key disappears; mounting v2 reactivates them against the new provider; final teardown recovers the initial context exactly.

demo_hot_swap.rb

demo_hot_swap.rb · lines 1–67

1# frozen_string_literal: true
2
3# Hot module replacement, the paper's capstone scenario (§1.2, §5.2.2), on the
4# Cordis runtime: components declare coeffect specifications (Definition 25),
5# every context transition is classified against them (Definition 26), and
6# unloading recovers the shared context exactly (revertible effects, §3.1).
7#
8# Run: ruby demo_hot_swap.rb

10require_relative "cordis"

12system = Cordis::System.new
13
14# A component's requires is its coeffect specification d ∈ 𝔇_Σ (Definition 25);
15# its activation block performs witnessed set effects (Definition 23), each
16# inverse joining the component's own accumulator (§3.1.3).
17logger_v1 = Cordis::Component.new("logger@1.0", requires: [:config]) do |ctx|

19  ctx.provide(:log, ->(msg) { "#{prefix} #{msg}" })

24  ctx.provide(:log, ->(msg) { "#{prefix} ⚡ #{msg.upcase}" })
25end

30end
31
32dashboard = Cordis::Component.new("dashboard", requires: %i[log count_events]) do |ctx|

35end

39raise "nothing should be active" unless system.active.empty?

  mount    dashboard  (requires [:log, :count_events])
  mount    logger@1.0  (requires [:config])
  effect   set(:config)
  activate logger@1.0
  effect   set(:log)
  activate metrics
  effect   set(:count_events)
  activate dashboard

  deactivate dashboard

  mount    logger@2.0  (requires [:config])
  activate logger@2.0
  effect   set(:log)
  activate metrics
  effect   set(:count_events)
  activate dashboard
  effect   set(:render)
  deactivate logger@2.0
  deactivate metrics
  deactivate dashboard
  effect   revert set(:render)
  effect   revert set(:count_events)
  effect   revert set(:log)
  effect   revert set(:config)
  unmount  metrics
  unmount  dashboard
  unmount  logger@2.0

Reconciliation, declaratively

The same dependency tree, run through the loader instead of by hand: the orchestrator edits a record of entries; the calculus discovers activation order, rebuilds what changed, and — Theorem 73's licence — lands exactly where a from-scratch load of the final record would.

demo_reconcile.rb

demo_reconcile.rb · lines 1–69

1# frozen_string_literal: true
2
3# Declarative configuration over the fiber calculus (§5.2.1): the
4# orchestrator edits a persistent record of entries (Definition 74); the
5# loader reconciles the running system to it, and Theorem 73 guarantees the
6# result is the one a from-scratch load of the final record would produce.
7#
8# Run: ruby demo_reconcile.rb
9
10require_relative "cordis_loader"
11
12def entry(id, builder, config: nil, disabled: false)
13  Cordis::Loader::Entry.new(id: id, builder: builder, config: config, disabled: disabled)
14end
15
16CONFIG = ->(_) { Cordis::Calculus::Component.build(:config_provider, provide: [:config]) { |c| c.set(:config, { prefix: "[core]" }) } }
17LOGGER = lambda do |version|
18  Cordis::Calculus::Component.build(:logger, inject: [:config], provide: [:log]) do |c|
19    c.set(:log) { |reads| "#{reads[:config][:prefix]} v#{version}" }
20  end
21end
22METRICS = lambda do |_|
23  Cordis::Calculus::Component.build(:metrics, inject: [:log], provide: [:count]) do |c|
24    c.set(:count) { |reads| "count via #{reads[:log]}" }
25  end
26end
27DASH = lambda do |_|
28  Cordis::Calculus::Component.build(:dashboard, inject: %i[log count], provide: [:render]) do |c|
29    c.set(:render) { |reads| "render(#{reads[:count]})" }
30  end
31end
32
33def tree(logger_version:, dash_disabled: false)
34  [entry("config", CONFIG), entry("logger", LOGGER, config: logger_version),
35   entry("metrics", METRICS), entry("dash", DASH, disabled: dash_disabled)]
36end
37
38loader = Cordis::Loader::Reconciler.new(Cordis::Calculus::Machine.new(seed: 42))
39
40puts "— Reconcile v1: four entries, dependency order discovered reactively (Theorem 63)"
41loader.reconcile(tree(logger_version: "1.0"))
42puts "  render: #{loader.machine.sigma[:render]}"
43
44puts "— Reconcile v2: logger config 1.0 → 2.0 (entry rebuilt), dash disabled (fiber unloaded)"
45loader.reconcile(tree(logger_version: "2.0", dash_disabled: true))
46puts "  count:  #{loader.machine.sigma[:count]}"
47puts "  render? #{loader.machine.sigma.key?(:render)}"
48
49puts "— Reconcile v3: dash re-enabled — reloads against the new logger, nothing else moves"
50loader.reconcile(tree(logger_version: "2.0"))
51puts "  render: #{loader.machine.sigma[:render]}"
52
53puts "— The licence (Theorem 73): the reconciled system equals a from-scratch load of v3"
54fresh = Cordis::Loader::Reconciler.new(Cordis::Calculus::Machine.new(seed: 7))
55fresh.reconcile(tree(logger_version: "2.0"))
56raise "reconciliation diverged from static assembly!" unless fresh.machine.snapshot == loader.machine.snapshot
57
58puts "  snapshots equal — dynamic history left no trace"
59
60puts "— Reconcile ∅: every entry withdrawn; the registry empties"
61loader.reconcile([])
62raise "fibers remain!" unless loader.machine.fibers.empty?
63raise "context remains!" unless loader.machine.sigma.empty?
64
65puts "  registry empty, derived context empty"
66
67puts
68puts "Rule trace of the v1→v2 reconciliation window (each line is one rule application):"
69loader.machine.trace.each { |rule, name| puts "  #{rule.to_s.ljust(9)} #{name}" }

Colophon

Provenance & running the code

Paper: Yifan Shi, Wei Zhang, Tianyi Cui, A Programming Paradigm for Spatiotemporal Composability (Peking University / DeepSeek-AI). Reproduced here in full — all eight sections, 88 pages — for study alongside its implementation; all definitions, theorems, equation, rule, algorithm, and figure numbers are the paper's own. (An earlier revision of this guide covered only pages 1–21, mistaking the file for an excerpt; this revision reads and reproduces the whole paper.) Ruby: written for this guide, stdlib only, in docs/papers/spatiotemporal-composability/ of the recurgent repository. The code sections above are generated from the committed sources by build.rb, which also verifies that every trace link on this page resolves.

ruby theorem_checks.rb
the 28 checks of §3's theorems and the runtime's failure discipline
ruby calculus_checks.rb
the 14 checks of §4's metatheory and §5.2's loader, over randomized schedules
ruby demo_hot_swap.rb
reactive hot module replacement on the §3 runtime, with the transition trace
ruby demo_reconcile.rb
declarative reconciliation on the §4 calculus, with the rule trace
ruby build.rb
regenerates index.html from guide.template.html + paper/*.html + sources + fresh runs

Why it's in this repository: recurgent's Agent objects are the paper's motivating example made concrete — an agent harness that composes capabilities at runtime (§1.2.2). A child Agent that registers tools, spawns delegates, or mutates shared state is performing exactly the dynamic composition the paper formalizes; witnessed effects and declared coeffects are a candidate discipline for making delegation trees revertible and delegation dependencies reactive.

Built from the committed sources · every reference on this page is checked at build time · serif voice = the paper, sans voice = this guide, mono voice = code that runs.
